# nUIget UX overhaul — research report

Date: 2026-10-23 · Tree: `main` at `e4d6e97` (2.0.0 merged, unpublished) · Author: the planning session (Fable 5.1)
with 14 Opus research agents, one ExTester probe, 54 Fable refuters, a completeness critic, a floor analysis and a
Modern UI verification.

How to read: §1 is the answer. §2–§16 are the dimensions of the owner's brief, each with the verified facts, the
findings' ids and what they decide. Appendix A is the findings ledger (every row has an id, evidence and its
verification status); Appendix B the 72 design rules bound to measured numbers; Appendix C the triage of every 2.0.1
candidate; Appendices D–F the critic, the floor options and the Modern UI check. The per-dimension reports, the probe
output and the verification transcripts are the research bundle next to this file.

Method in one paragraph. Fourteen read-only Opus agents each took one dimension of the brief and wrote a report with
`path:line` or URL evidence; a probe test ran ExTester locally on VS Code 1.142.0 and dumped the resolved
`--vscode-*` variables under six themes, the rects and computed styles of every nUIget element in three states, and the
same measurements of the native Extensions view, pane headers, Settings editor, Search view, quick input and editor
tabs; an Opus agent bound the 72 design rules to those numbers. Every finding that would change a decision (175 of
299) then went through three Fable refuters, one per lens: does the cited evidence say this, what contradicts it, and
does the proposed consequence follow. The refuters corrected 155 claims, confirmed 17 unchanged, refuted one proposal
(X33) and left 2 to the owner (X34, V38); the corrections are folded into this text and recorded per row in Appendix A.
A completeness critic, a floor analysis and a Modern UI verification followed (Appendices D–F). Model split: Opus for
the sweep and the binding, Fable for verification and synthesis.

## 1. Answer first

1. **"Good" is the workbench's own numbers, and the tree misses most of them today.** The design research derived 72
   measurable rules (`DS-*`) from VS Code's source and the UX guidelines, 70 of them measurable by a script; bound to
   the probe, 37 fail on today's tree, 8 pass and 27 need states the probe did not capture (Appendix B). V1–V5 are
   all measurable, with numbers (§8): the tab strip is 25 px against 35 px workbench strips and controls are 24 px
   against 26 px (V1); the toolbar pads 16 px start and 6 px end against rows that pad 16 px both sides (V2); a
   section header's action slot sits on the list's right edge (V3); a section header has 4 px above its box and
   12 px below (V4); control gaps are {0, 4, 5, 8} with a 7 px stray (V5).
2. **The workbench now has two looks, and the design follows the host** (§3, Appendix F). VS Code's "Modern UI"
   (`workbench.experimental.modernUI`) is the compiled default in every stable release from 1.139 (an experiment may
   assign it off per user), so a user on a current VS Code sees 28 px capitalised pane headers, rounded rows and 8 px
   scrollbars by default; ExTester forces the classic look (`modernUI: false`, `--disable-experiments`), so every
   capture so far, CI or local, is classic. The Design System therefore aliases the exported size tokens with the
   classic values as fallbacks and switches the few metrics that differ (pane header 22→28 px and 11 px/700
   uppercase→12 px/600 capitalised, header hover tint, row radius and inset, scrollbar 10→8 px, badge type) on a
   root attribute the host mirrors from the setting; the harness pins Modern UI explicitly as the primary goldens and
   keeps one classic pass. Classic reference numbers measured on 1.142.0 (Dark Modern): Extensions row 72 px with a
   36 px icon, tree and quick-input rows 22 px, pane header 22 px at 11 px/700 uppercase, select and button 26 px
   (12 px font, radius 4), input 25–26 px, check box 18 px (radius 3), count badge an 18 px pill, side-bar title and
   editor tabs 35 px; selection `list.activeSelectionBackground` with a 1 px `focusBorder` outline. A second probe
   run with `workbench.experimental.modernUI: true` written on top of ExTester's default rendered Modern UI on the
   same 1.142.0 (`.monaco-workbench` carries `modern-ui modern-ui-tabs modern-ui-connected-editor-tabs
   modern-ui-notifications-dialogs floating-panels modern-ui-frosted-glass`) and measured: pane header 28 px with a
   4 px radius and a 12 px/600 title with no text transform, tree rows 22 px with a 4 px radius, side-bar title 32 px,
   editor tab strip 29 px, quick-input radius 8 px, buttons 26 px at 12 px with radius 4 (unchanged), Extensions rows
   72 px (unchanged), the side bar inset 5 px from the activity bar (floating panels). So the metrics that differ
   between the looks are the pane header, the row radius, the part titles and the tab strip, and nothing in the rows
   or controls.
3. **Keep the hand-rolled primitives; do not adopt `@vscode-elements/elements`** (§6). The element set would add
   123 kB to the panel bundle and 52 kB to the sidebar against 26 kB and 17 kB of headroom, break the jsdom harness
   (no `ElementInternals`), slot actions inside `role=treeitem` against the listbox contract, and has one maintainer.
   Instead: regenerate the icon SVGs from the pinned `@vscode/codicons` drawings with a script (18 of the 23 codicon
   glyphs differ from any current drawing; the CC-BY attribution is missing), and rewrite the CSS against VS Code's
   exported size tokens with px fallbacks (radius, body font size, codicon size and stroke exported from 1.110.1, the
   spacing ramp from 1.124.0, the font-size and weight ramps from 1.129.0, none at 1.85). Cost 4–5.5 session-days
   plus a quarter day for the ADR-008 amendment the em→px type change needs.
4. **The debt sits around the primitives, not inside them** (§6). One rebuild (merge the two Updates tabs into the
   sections model the Installed tab has), 32 refactors, 13 keeps, six missing primitives (Button, Select, Checkbox,
   TextField, PropertyList, GroupCaption) and a typed row adapter over `Row`. 17 feature-stylesheet rules override 9
   primitive classes (12 of them added by P9); a 116-entry lint baseline (58 literal lengths, 41 over-specific
   selectors, 16 cross-file overrides, 1 duplicate selector) is exact and ready for the negative control. A lexical
   lint catches none of V1–V5; only geometry does. Floor estimate 15.5 planned days, plus 1–1.5 for the main-panel
   context menu and 7 more if the plan chooses one grouped list per view.
5. **The harness is DOM geometry first** (§5). Rects measured inside the real webview were bit-identical across three
   CI runs on two runner images; pixel diff of the webview crop is 0 px on 13 of 15 states and becomes a hard 0 px gate
   only with live content masked and the VS Code version pinned; vision judges cannot see 2–8 px offsets and stay
   judge-only. The current P9.fix-4 gate tolerates 8 px where V2 is 6 px by design: tolerances become 1 px for edges
   meant to coincide. ExTester 8.28.1 supports VS Code 1.138–1.140 and `max` floats with Dependabot; pin the version.
6. **Interaction defects the design must fix, not restyle** (§7, §9): the main panel has no actions menu although its
   hint promises Shift+F10 (the workbench's `webview/context` menu with `data-vscode-context` on the row wrapper
   exists at 1.85, no protocol change); Enter writes without confirmation on sidebar Installed, sidebar Browse and
   main Updates rows while the Extensions view's Enter opens; Delete uninstalls at once where the Explorer confirms;
   focus falls to `<body>` after confirming a bulk dialog; VS Code's `body.vscode-reduce-motion` is ignored; every
   single success raises a toast where the workbench confirms in place (D17 reopened).
7. **The functional gaps are the vulnerability and deprecation workflow** (§10): when no allowed source offers
   vulnerability data the tree records "no vulnerabilities" instead of "unknown" (against the repository's own
   "empty is not unknown" rule); transitive vulnerabilities are never counted; deprecation is shown nowhere and has
   no protocol field; no version is marked vulnerable in the selector. Consolidate across projects, promote a
   transitive package, palette commands, mapping visibility and editor signals follow, each costed.
8. **There is no external user signal** (§11): one issue, 0 reviews, 83 installs, Discussions off. The 1.x
   CHANGELOG is the signal: 199 Fixed headlines, the sidebar in 61 of them, cross-surface sync broken 8 times, stuck
   spinners 10 times, All Projects the largest recurring theme. The owner's three 2.0 reviews narrowed from content to
   layout to spacing; the P9.fix gates measured edges and the owner then filed V2/V4/V5 on the same elements: vertical
   rhythm and symmetry were never measured.
9. **The listing is the acquisition surface and needs its own rules** (§12): the Marketplace still serves 1.18.9;
   the README carries 20 contributor-only lines and no image below its hero; vsce refuses `<svg>`, `data:` images and
   non-HTTPS images; the README's images point at the mutable `main` branch; the reproducible preview is VS Code's own
   Extensions view in ExTester, not a re-implemented renderer; global Azure DevOps PATs retire on 2026-12-01, so the
   release checklist's `vsce publish` route changes before the pre-release.
10. **Decisions the design must take, as alternatives for the owner's rounds** (§17): two-line 72 px rows with the
    Extensions anatomy versus the 78 px three-line card (D15); 22 px compact rows versus the 24 px pointer target;
    one grouped list per view versus per-section lists; what Enter "opens" in a sidebar without a details pane; no
    toast on single success (D17); the VS Code floor (Appendix E). Everything else is decided by the
    closest-to-the-workbench rule and recorded.

Not reachable, by the agents' own lists: Thunder Client and Database Client sources (closed), Jupyter's webview CSS,
Rider's page only through a fetch summary, npm/pnpm GUIs and cargo tools (not covered), the Marketplace's server-side
renderer (closed), the Marketplace Q&A beyond its counts, VS Code version distribution among users (no public data
found). The brief's premise that the captures came from "a 1.105-era build" was wrong: they are 1.140.0.

## 4. What good looks like: the measurable rules

The rules are in Appendix B with their bound numbers; the Design System (`docs/design/design-system.md`) carries their
full text. The areas and the exemplar each binds to:

| Area | Rules | Exemplar and the numbers that bind (classic 1.142.0, probe) | Today |
|---|---|---|---|
| GRID | DS-GRID-1..3 | the spacing ramp {0,2,4,6,8,10,12,16,20,24,28,32,36,40}; block gaps of at most two values; tokens alias `--vscode-spacing-*` with px fallbacks | tokens are literal; gaps {0,4,5,7,8} |
| INSET | DS-INSET-1..4 | one 16 px content inset (Extensions row padding-left); symmetric start/end and top/bottom insets on every header, toolbar and control row; trailing badges keep ≥ 12 px (sidebar) or the inset (main); details header 20 px | V2 (16/6), V3 (0), V5 (8/4) |
| HEIGHT | DS-HEIGHT-1..11 | pane header 22; compact row 22; comfortable row 72; tab strip ≈ 33–36; details tab strip 36; text button 26; inputs 25–26; icon button 22 hover box in a ≥ 24 target; check box 18; badge 18 pill; progress 2 | 25, 24, 78, 25, 25, 24, 24, 24 (no hover box), 14/16, 17×16 radius 4, 2 |
| ROW | DS-ROW-1..6 | Extensions anatomy (16 px start, 36 px icon, centred text column); one icon x and text x per scroll container; hover/selection/focus over the whole row, actions included; ≤ 3 actions on hover and focus; the name truncates last; priority hiding at the Extensions breakpoints (icon 24 at ≤ 250, hidden at ≤ 200) | 32 px icon; transitive and Browse rows 26 px left of Installed rows; the band stops before the actions |
| TYPE | DS-TYPE-1..7 | `--vscode-font-family` only; the ramp {10,11,12,13,18,26}; weights 400/600 (700 on pane headers); 1.4 line height; pane-header casing from the host; secondary text 11–12 px; details title 18 px | 0.85em compounding to 9.4 px badges; 14.95 px title; UA-default 700 on details headings |
| ICON | DS-ICON-1..5 | the pinned codicon release (a host-matched release is unsatisfiable: 1.85 hosts draw the pre-redraw glyphs, 1.142 the redrawn ones), 16 px in `icon.foreground`, 12 px inline; Verified = `verified-filled` in `extensionIcon.verifiedForeground`; package icon per the Extensions breakpoints | 5 glyphs pre-redraw, 5 redrawn, 13 hand variants of no release; the Verified mark is a 14 px check in the link colour |
| COLOR | DS-COLOR-1..7 | the role table (§5 of the rules report): selection follows list focus (`inactiveSelection*` when unfocused); icon buttons hover `toolbar.hoverBackground`; tabs use `panelTitle.*`; `focusBorder` marks focus only; `disabledForeground`; secondary buttons hover | 13 roles absent from `tokens.css`; `focusBorder` doubles as accent and tab underline; hover beats selection |
| DENSITY | DS-DENSITY-1..2 | per surface, no toggle; Modern UI compact density judge-only | holds |
| OVERFLOW | DS-OVERFLOW-1..5 | no horizontal scroll at 170 px (sidebar) and 220 px (editor); no sibling collision; a declared min width and overflow behaviour per control container; ≤ 4 sidebar title actions; split bounds in px | 6 title actions; the details action grid cannot wrap; split clamped in percent |
| STATE | DS-STATE-1..7 | a defined loading/revalidating/empty/error/partial/unsupported cell per screen with a marker and a capture; progress where the work is (2 px bar); welcome-style empty states; inline errors; in-place success; `-ing` labels; every disabled affordance says why | 13 ready-state captures + 2 state cells; success toasts; centred empty states |
| FOCUS | DS-FOCUS-1..4 | 1 px rings, offsets per control; `list.focusOutline` on rows; HC dotted/dashed outlines; keyboard-only is a design choice (the classic list outlines a clicked row too) | `focusBorder` 1 px −1 everywhere |
| MOTION | DS-MOTION-1..2 | 100–150 ms ease-out; spinner 1.5 s steps(30); progress 4 s; reduced motion from both the OS and `body.vscode-reduce-motion` | 200 ms; 1 s; 1.6 s; OS signal only |
| COPY | DS-COPY-1..5 | Title Case labels, sentence-case messages; no colons on label–value pairs; sentences end with a period; `...` for commands that open UI; one count style per surface | mixed casing, `Author(s)`/`Authors`, U+2026 |
| DIVIDER | DS-DIVIDER-1..4 | no line between rows; elevation only on layers; quick-pick separators in popups; one divider role per surface | a line under every card; `widget.border` (#313131) beside the workbench's #2b2b2b |

Three rule premises are design choices rather than measurements, and the Design System decides them by the
closest-to-the-workbench rule: the block-gap set (the Extensions view measures 5 and 10, the ramp's base is 4), symmetric
insets (the Extensions header pads 5/12/6/20), and keyboard-only focus rings (the classic list paints a 1 px outline
after a click; Modern UI's `keyboardFocusOnly` module does not). Row heights stay literal tokens (72/22, 28 under
Modern UI headers) while the rest of the scale aliases the exported tokens. Two bound verdicts are vacuous and count as
not measurable: DS-COLOR-2 (only the focused half was probed; by source the rule fails) and DS-OVERFLOW-2 (the V3 state
was not in the probe). DS-HEIGHT-3 binds to the row pitch including the divider (79 px today), not the token.

Each of V1–V5 maps to rules that fail today: V1 → DS-HEIGHT-4/6/7, DS-GRID-2; V2 → DS-INSET-2/3, DS-ROW-3;
V3 → DS-INSET-3, DS-OVERFLOW-2; V4 → DS-GRID-2(b); V5 → DS-GRID-1/2, DS-INSET-2. V1's residual ("still looks
squeezed" after the heights match) is the one judge-only part.

## 5. Measurement: what a script can judge, ranked

| Rank | Technique | Catches | Gate | Build / run cost | Verified facts |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | DOM relational geometry inside the webview (alignment, centring, containment, gap equality and proximity, truncation, overlap) | V2, V3, V5, V4 (proximity rule), P9.fix-2..6; V1 with rank 2 | hard | 1.5–2.5 d / +0.1–0.3 s per state | every `[P9.*]` number identical across three CI runs on ubuntu-24.04 and 26.04 |
| 2 | Workbench-exemplar parity in the same session (heights, fonts, colours of the Extensions view, pane headers, Settings controls, quick input) | V1; the native feel of every height | hard for heights and colours | 1 d / +2–5 s | the probe did it on this Mac; the P0 step repeats it on the CI lane |
| 3 | axe-core in the real webview, full A/AA ruleset per state and theme | a11y regressions; ~12 page-level rules jsdom never decides | hard (violations) | 0.5 d / 1–2 s per surface | the theme pass runs two rules today; jsdom decided 41 of 86 rules |
| 4 | Keyboard walk and accessibility snapshot per state (`getAriaRole`, `getAccessibleName`, `activeElement` from inside the frame) | focus order, names, the §5 key map | hard | 1 d | selenium-webdriver 4.51 exposes the calls; CDP into the webview frame is unverified, so no `getFullAXTree` promise |
| 5 | Pixel diff (pixelmatch 8, `includeAA: false`, threshold 0.1) of the webview crop against a pinned golden | any unintended change | informational, then hard at 0 px with masks and a pinned VS Code | 1–1.5 d (+1–2 for a fixture feed) | 13 of 15 states 0 px between runs; the rest is live nuget.org content, the caret, toasts and one anti-aliasing switch |
| 6 | Ink profiles of the crop (gap and alignment lines) | V1/V4/V5 as perceived | informational | 0.5–1 d | the tool exists in the bundle (`research/tools/`) |
| 7 | Chrome layout shift across states | P9.fix-2 class | hard for chrome | 0.5 d | — |
| 8 | Theme × width matrix | multiplies 1–6 | inherits | 0.5 d / ~4–5 s per theme switch | the sidebar sash drag exists (`sidebar-narrow-200px`) |
| 9 | SSIM | nothing beyond 5 | do not adopt | — | a 6 px shift barely moves the score |
| 10 | Vision judges (rubric, several judges, refuter, coordinates) | holistic "reads as built-in"; not 2–8 px offsets | judge-only | 1 d / ~1.5k tokens per PNG | localisation documented as approximate |

Tolerances for the plan: 1 px for edges meant to coincide (the current 8 px admits V2), 1 px for centring, 0 px
clearance for containment, equal same-role gaps within 1 px, workbench parity within 1 px for heights and equal computed
colours, `scrollWidth ≤ clientWidth` for named text, chrome rects equal across states within 0.5 px, 0 mismatched
pixels for the masked crop, 0 axe violations with `incomplete` allowed only in the details pane.

Harness facts the plan relies on: `VSBrowser.takeScreenshot` returns the whole window (crop with the outer iframe rect
from the top document); `extest setup-and-run` always packages the working tree, so the two-tree negative control uses
the split commands (`get-vscode`, `get-chromedriver`, `install-vsix -f <vsix> -e`, `run-tests`); `actions/cache` on the
VS Code download saves ~19 s and buys version stability, not speed; `setup-dotnet` takes a multi-line
`dotnet-version` (8.0.x and 10.0.x) and an Unsupported fixture needs the exact patch its `global.json` pins (a failed
resolution makes the project *supported* today, so the fixture must be checked by the harness); reduced motion needs no
CDP once the product honours `body.vscode-reduce-motion`.

## 6. The webview code: what to keep, refactor or rebuild

**Component base.** Keep the primitives. `@vscode-elements/elements` 2.5.1 (MIT, Lit 3.3.3) has no listbox/option and
no virtualization; its tree item slots actions inside the `treeitem`; its toolbar and icon buttons render inner
shadow `<button>`s that cannot take `tabIndex -1`; it builds at `chrome114` but neither it nor Lit declares that
floor; jsdom 30 lacks `ElementInternals.setFormValue` (16 call sites) so 10 test files and 53 queries break for sure;
the measured bundle delta is +123,471 / +51,954 bytes against 26,372 / 17,332 of headroom. Option C instead: inline
SVG icons generated from the pinned codicon drawings by a script with a lint that compares paths (18 of 23 named
glyphs change; two custom glyphs map to codicons; the 14 px Verified glyph snaps to 16/12), the CC-BY-4.0 attribution
added to LICENSE/README, and the stylesheet rewritten against the exported size tokens with px fallbacks (type moves
from em to px, which amends ADR-008). 4–5.5 session-days plus 0.25–0.5 for the ADR item.

**CSS audit** (`css-audit.md`, baseline in `css-audit-work/baseline.json`, sha256 `4417610d…`). 2,054 lines, 956
declarations, spacing and colour fully tokenised. The six candidate lint rules hit exactly 116 places: `pxLength` 58
(34 of them 1 px lines, 8 pinned verbatim by existing test lines; README typography; em widths; 11 `font-weight: 600`
with no token), `duplicateSelector` 1, `specificity` 41 (35 with three or more classes; max (0,5,0)), `override` 16
(main.css 9, sidebar.css 7; 12 introduced by P9), `deadRule` 0, `disableComment` 0. Seventeen feature rules set
non-placement properties on nine primitive classes (SectionHeader takes nine). Two cascade defects against VS Code's
list widget: hover beats selection in every single-select list, and there is no inactive-selection colour. Controls
(button, three text inputs, select, check box) have no primitive. The Popover's CSS ships in both bundles while its JS
is tree-shaken. Verdict: keep `tokens.css`, refactor `primitives.css` with the missing variants, delete the per-surface
patches; not a token rebuild. Bundle cascade order is primitives, tokens, surface stylesheet, then `search.css`.

**Component audit** (`component-audit.md`, 46 units). One rebuild: the Updates tab, consolidated into the Installed
tab's sections model after the list-model decision and the outer-scroller virtualization (1.5 d). 32 refactors, 13
keeps. The debt around the primitives: the `Listbox` "always virtualized" contract is broken by `main.css:206-222`
(every row mounted in the Installed tab and All Projects Updates; refactor 1.5–2 d, superseding the P9.fix-2 ruling's
DO-NOT); `Listbox` reads `Row` by DOM structure and class; eight distinct row implementations across nine `renderRow`
props with five copies of `busyStatus`; six missing primitives (20 raw `nuiget-button`, 4 raw selects, 6 raw check
boxes); the main lists have no context menu. `Row` is a refactor (its triad is pinned by the listbox contract, §8 and
ExTester) plus a typed adapter. Tests couple to roles and test ids (443 `*ByRole`, 440 `*ByTestId`, 0 snapshots, 94
class selectors, 26 ExTester class reads in 8 files); 18 computed-style reads pin rule text and break under refactor as
well. Cost: 15.5 planned days as a floor (13.5 + 2.0 new primitives and adapter), +1–1.5 d for the menu, +7 d under a
grouped single list (Listbox 3 d and eight consumers); every unit rebuilt would be 45 d. The P9 git log measures
0.76 h per item (12 items in 9.1 h); the brief's 1.1 h per planned UI day is the rate the plan uses.

Order by screen: decide the list model; `Listbox` + row adapter (unblocks Installed, Updates, Browse, sidebar);
`SectionHeader`, `Toolbar`, `Tabs`, `Badge` variants (V1–V5); the Updates merge; Button, Select (a native wrapper
0.25 d, an optional custom dropdown 1.5–2 d for Windows/Linux), Checkbox, TextField, PropertyList, GroupCaption;
ConfirmDialog list API, one `CentralUpdateDialog` mount, one `useInstallAction`; a shell chrome hook shared by
`MainShell` (308 lines, over ADR-008's 300) and `SidebarView`.

## 7. The UX as it is: inventory and deviations

`ux-inventory.md` holds the screen × state matrix (component, test id and capture per cell), the action table, the IA
trees with control sizes, and 890 strings. Twenty deviations from `docs/ux/interaction-model.md`, the ones that change
the design:

- **No actions menu in the main panel** (X1, A3, X12, X18): no main list passes `onContextMenu`, right-click is
  swallowed by `Listbox`'s `preventDefault`, and the Installed hint tells screen-reader users that Shift+F10 opens a
  menu. Only the sidebar's QuickPick menu exists. The workbench route is `contributes.menus["webview/context"]` with
  `data-vscode-context` on the row wrapper (available at 1.85; the command receives the merged context plus a
  `webview` field); a queued operation started from a native menu is not marked busy today (X25: a host-level progress
  emitter for queued operations fixes that without a contract change). 1–1.5 d.
- **Enter writes** (A1, A2, X13): sidebar Installed Enter uninstalls, sidebar Browse Enter installs, main Updates Enter
  updates (documented in README, against §5 of the model). The Extensions view's Enter opens the item. The design
  moves writes to Ctrl+Enter (the 1.x binding), Delete and the menu; what "open" means in a sidebar without a details
  pane is the owner's choice (today's "open full view", or select only).
- **Delete is unconfirmed** on both surfaces (X14) where the Explorer confirms with "Do not ask me again".
- **The sidebar never shows the source name** (X15); the single-view container also hides the native view description
  lever, so the choice is the title string or a header line in the webview (0.25 d).
- **Cross-surface navigation lands in Browse** (X17, X26): "View package details" on an installed row opens the main
  panel in Browse mode. The sidebar's menu route can reach the right tab today through `ContextMenuRequest` and
  `ui/selectProject`; the direct row request needs `projectPath?` on `ui/viewPackage` (a contract field).
- **Restore exists only in the transitive banners** (X39, G10) while §5 lists it in the header.
- **Every success is reported three times** (X40): status bar, toast, live region (four for bulk, with the progress
  notification). The workbench confirms in place and announces; the design keeps the warning toasts and the bulk
  summary and drops the single-success toast (D17, owner).
- **No keybindings; every command title carries "nUIget: "** instead of a category (X16).
- **The captures show 13 ready states, a provisional badge and one dialog** (X41, H20): no loading, revalidating,
  error, unavailable, unsupported, unresolved, CPM, busy, stale-details, filter-mode, multi-source, All Projects
  sidebar, QuickPick, host modal or narrow-main state has ever been captured. The deterministic cells become fixture
  projects and named states in P0.

## 8. Visual audit of the current tree

V1–V5 by numbers (probe, `main-installed-single`, CSS px; the ledger rows V37, V41–V44, V52–V55 give the CSS):

| Finding | Measured | Cause | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| V1 squeezed | tab strip 25 px (editor tabs 35); buttons, inputs, selects 24 px (workbench 26 / 25–26) with a 13 px font (12) and radius 2 (4); 4 px above and below the toolbar buttons | `--nuiget-target-min` (24 px) is the drawn height of 22 controls; no vertical padding on the tab list | DS-HEIGHT-4/6/7, DS-GRID-2 |
| V2 misaligned | toolbar start inset 16, end inset 6 (`Uninstall selected` ends at 925, the list at 931); the selected row's band ends at 907, its actions at 907–931 outside it | `main.css:190-195` overrides the toolbar's end padding; the band is painted on the option, the actions are a sibling | DS-INSET-2/3, DS-ROW-3 |
| V3 badge on the border | `.nuiget-section-actions` sits at x 931 with width 0; the sidebar's Update all ends at 300, the pane edge | neither `.nuiget-section-header` nor `.nuiget-section-actions` has end padding; badges are passed as header actions | DS-INSET-3, DS-OVERFLOW-2 |
| V4 header margins | 4 px above the header box, 12.4 below; by text line boxes 8.3 above, 16.7 below; the native pane header is 10 above, 6.4 below | the header's half-leading stacks on the card's 12 px top padding; no header padding of its own | DS-GRID-2(b) |
| V5 no scale | control gaps {0, 4, 5, 8}, a 7 px `padding-left` on the tab bar, header padding 8 top / 4 bottom; ink gaps 13, 10, 11, 22 | five unrelated paddings | DS-GRID-1/2, DS-INSET-2 |

Two corrections from the refuters: `--nuiget-target-min` is the `min-height` of 15 rules (11 real controls), it is a
floor that allows 26 px, so the cause of V1 is the missing vertical padding, not the token (V14); and the 33 px
Settings-tab reference for the tab strip is CSS-read, so the tab strip's deviation is measured against the 35 px
editor tab strip until the probe binds `.settings-tabs-widget` and the Extensions editor navbar (V37).

Beyond V1–V5, the audit found: on VS Code 1.90 and later (Chromium 122) the host's `html { scrollbar-color }` overrides
every `::-webkit-scrollbar` rule, so the token-coloured 10 px scrollbars are inert on every current host and users see
the 15 px classic bar; the fix is a `scrollbar-color: auto` reset on the root beside the WebKit rules, admitted by a
documented progressive-enhancement entry in the CSS-baseline lint, and it needs no floor change (V15); the sash is a
painted 4 px bar that turns `focusBorder` on hover at once where the workbench draws a 1 px separator and a transparent
sash that highlights after 300 ms (V16; `sash.hoverBorder` equals `focusBorder` in every built-in theme, so the role is
the mismatch); em nesting renders sidebar count badges at 9.4 px and bold (V17; the rule becomes "a relative size goes
on leaf text, never on a container that holds a Badge"); the row check mark is a 14 px box in the divider colour at
1.0–1.5:1 contrast (V18, A7; one 18 px check-box visual in the checkbox tokens follows, the 3:1 trade-off is the
owner's); the dialog scrim is the editor background at 60 %, which lightens the sidebar in both Modern themes and
darkens only where the editor is darker (V19; a literal black scrim in `tokens.css` with a one-line widening of the
tokens lint); the sidebar confirms Update all in-panel and central updates with a host modal, both documented, and
unifying them needs a host-confirmed request behind the sentinel (X9, about one session-day); disabled primary buttons
are restyled as secondary and become invisible in Dark Modern, and on Light Modern the enabled and disabled buttons are
identical in every respect (V38; the owner chooses between opacity 0.4 and `disabledForeground`); comfortable rows
measure 79 px because the border adds to `min-height: inherit` (V39); high-contrast tabs are boxed and count badges
lose their box (V33, V56); the details tab labels start 7 px left of the details title (V57); Browse and transitive
rows start 26 px left of Installed rows (V58, V45); the details facts' value column is ragged (V59); sidebar titles
start at x 72 against the native 68 (V60); details headings fall back to UA-default 700 (V61); the quick-search popup
draws text glyphs for chevrons (V62); nUIget lines use `widget.border` beside workbench lines in another grey (V63).

## 9. Accessibility

The primitives are sound in structure (one tab stop per list, every row action mirrored by a key, dialogs that trap and
restore focus, one live region per surface, a six-theme contrast and target-size pass with zero violations). Verified
defects: focus falls to `<body>` after confirming a bulk uninstall or Update all because the opener is disabled by the
same update (A6; reproduced in jsdom on the real tabs); Enter is destructive in three lists (A2); the main panel's hint
promises a menu that does not exist (A3); `body.vscode-reduce-motion` is ignored (A4; VS Code sets it since 1.85 and
ExTester turns it on, so every CI capture is a reduced-motion capture that still animates); the row check box's border
measures 1.03–1.48:1 (A7); README syntax colours alias icon colours at 2.55–2.89:1 in the light themes (A8); the
new-version colour falls below 4.5:1 on hover in the light themes (A14); the main panel's first load announces nothing
(A9); the empty-list focus target has no outline and no role (A15); links take the UA focus ring (A16); README headings
keep their Markdown level inside the pane (A17); the search combobox does not describe its hidden keys (A18); in Dark
High Contrast a selected compact row may have no mark but the focus ring (A19); Multi Install's "why not pickable"
reason is not tied to the check box (A20).

Coverage: the jsdom suite ran axe 60 times over 86 rules and decided 41; the real webview runs `color-contrast` and
`target-size` only, with `incomplete` nodes inside the details pane allowed through in every theme (A5). The screen-reader
pass (P7.4) stays out of scope and is recorded as such (A21 lists what only a screen reader can settle).

Automated substitutes the plan adopts (H46–H49, H86–H88): an accessible-tree snapshot per named state from inside the
webview (axe's own `getRole` and `accessibleText`, not CDP); the full axe ruleset plus `label-content-name-mismatch` and
`target-size` per state with every layer open; ExTester keyboard paths from §5 (tab order against an expected sequence,
the row keys, dialog trap/Escape/restore); a jsdom guard that `document.activeElement` is never `<body>` after any
close; a runtime reduced-motion check under `workbench.reduceMotion: on`; contrast measured in hover, open-layer and
README states plus an offline theme lint over the token pairs; a rule-coverage ledger so a pinned axe rule cannot
silently become inapplicable.

## 10. Feature gaps

Where nUIget leads (keep and market): package-source mapping applied to every metadata read (Visual Studio's UI does
not, NuGet/Home#12783); DPAPI and credential-provider credentials; CPM edits with an impact preview; a provisional list
from the project files replaced by the evaluated one; unreachable sources reported as such; VS Code proxy support, no
telemetry, accessibility.

| Gap | What | Cost | Contract |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1 ⚑ | "No vulnerabilities" recorded when no allowed source offers vulnerability data; `@vulnerable` then says none (`vulnerability.ts:107-122`, `enricher.ts:71-73`); per-entry unknown state plus a frontend test | 0.5 d | none |
| G2 | Transitive vulnerabilities hidden until the section expands; never counted; `NuGetAuditMode` defaults to all on net10 | 1 d (count, filter, auto-open; All Projects via `enableTransitive`) | none |
| G3 | Deprecation shown nowhere, no protocol field; the search response already carries the latest version's deprecation (prerelease-inclusive), the installed version's needs a registration read per enriched id | one D6 item with G4 | yes (domain.ts) |
| G4 | No remediation path: versions not marked vulnerable in the selector, update rows do not say they fix an advisory; tri-state per-version flag (absent = unknown) | with G3 | yes |
| G5 | No Consolidate view for version drift across projects; `ops/bulkUpdateAllProjects` already takes a version; central rows excluded, overrides join | 1.5 d, under All Projects | none |
| G6 | A transitive package cannot be promoted to a direct reference (a 17.3 Visual Studio experiment; one of six options in Microsoft's audit guide) | 0.75–1 d, single project | §7 decision row |
| G7 | No Command Palette workflows (C# Dev Kit, jmrog 1.65 M installs, nifty have them); six host-only commands with tests and docs | 1.5 d floor | manifest, docs:check |
| G8 | Source mapping enforced but invisible and uneditable; nothing writes a mapping on install | TBD | protocol + nuget.config writer |
| G9 | No CodeLens, inline hints, diagnostics or open-declaring-file although `definedIn` exists (C# Dev Kit's CodeLens is 11.x prerelease) | TBD | host API |
| G10 | Restore only from the transitive banners | 0.25 d | none |
| G11 | No solution scope (D2) | — | ADR-000 decision |
| G12–G21 | Browse paging, source editing, licence acceptance, owners/tags/release notes, update policies, cache management, operation preview, a keybinding, localization (D11), TFM-filtered versions | each ≤ 1 d except localization | mixed |

The parity audit's open rows are triaged in Appendix C; the ones the design touches become screen items (MI7/DP8
severity on badges with the workbench's `problems*Icon.foreground` roles, MI10/MU8 collapse/expand on the toolbar whose
width rule must budget them, SB7's keyboard bridge as Ctrl/Cmd+DownArrow like the Extensions view, SB9, SB10/V3/MI12
as one header decision, SB13, SB14, MB16, MB18, MB19, DP10 depending on the sidebar row design).

## 11. User signal

External: one issue (#11, unlisted versions offered as updates, fixed in 1.18.9; the reporter also called the version
deprecated), 0 Marketplace reviews and 0 Q&A questions, Discussions off, 0 stars, 83 installs, the listing at 1.18.9.
Everything else is the owner's dogfooding, which is therefore the authority on what to fix.

Internal: 413 CHANGELOG headlines across 1.x, 199 of them Fixed; the sidebar in 61 of 199, the main panel and sidebar
out of sync 8 times in 5 versions, stuck spinners 10 times in 7, All Projects 22 Fixed headlines in 7 versions (the
largest recurring theme; fourth by area after sidebar 61, refresh/sync 35, updates 34); seven IA decisions went through
three or more states (the Activity Bar badge, what Refresh clears, Browse as tab or section, …); version-correctness
bugs recur in 8 versions; theme-dependent invisibility recurs in both generations. 1.x moved toward workbench-native
visuals five times. The owner's 2.0 reviews narrowed from content (P9) to layout (P9.fix) to spacing and alignment
(V1–V5); the P9.fix gates measured two horizontal and three vertical quantities, none of them symmetry or rhythm, and
the owner then filed V2/V4/V5 on the same elements. Issue intake is stale: the forms name 20 source paths, 19 deleted;
blank issues are disabled.

## 12. The Marketplace listing

Today: the live listing is 1.18.9 (2026-10-10) with the 1.x hero GIF (1765×1108, 921 frames, 77.6 s, 3.8 MiB) linked
from the mutable `main` branch. The README on `main` (18.8 KiB) has 20 contributor-only lines (257–276), requirements
at line 246 of 280, no image in any of its 8 sections (the three PNGs sit at the top and show the e2e workspace, a
Sign In button and an empty details pane), no support section, no privacy statement although "no telemetry" is
verifiable, and a "Visual Studio-style" positioning in `description` and README that conflicts with the native
direction. The CHANGELOG 2.0.0 section has 115 bullets: 40 user-facing, 24 mixed, 51 developer-only, 31 naming a `src/`
path, 50 starting with the noise prefix "2.0 ".

Rules verified from source and docs:

- vsce 4.0.0 refuses an `<svg>` element and every `data:` image, non-HTTPS images, and `.svg` images from any host
  outside 32 trusted ones plus GitHub Actions badges; it rewrites relative links to `github.com/<repo>/blob/HEAD` and
  images to `raw/HEAD`; `--githubBranch <sha>` pins both, and absolute URLs with a tag pin without a flag.
- Three renderers: vsce validates (marked 18 + parse5) and ships markdown; VS Code's Extensions view renders with
  marked 14 + DOMPurify and a fixed allow-list (video/source/details/figure allowed; picture/svg/style not; `img-src
  https: data:`, `media-src https:`); the Marketplace's server-side renderer is closed and drops `align` on `<figure>`.
  The reproducible preview is a UI test that opens nUIget's own Details tab in the Extensions view and screenshots it
  in light and dark, not a re-implementation.
- The Marketplace page keeps `<video>` tags; the docs promise nothing. A GIF stays the hero; peers' true heroes run
  5.3–24 s (median 12 s) and ≤ 5 MB; `pngjs` 7 + `gifenc` 1.0.3 (both MIT, no dependencies) render frames to a GIF.
- `keywords`: the limit is 30 merged tags (keywords plus derived tags), nUIget has 14 with five near-duplicates and none
  for CPM, vulnerabilities or project types; the category list has 20 entries today; `qna` is absent so the Q&A tab is
  on (peers redirect it); the README pruning threshold is above 37 KiB.
- Global Azure DevOps PATs retire on 2026-12-01; afterwards publishing is `vsce publish --azure-credential`, `--oidc`
  from GitHub Actions, or the upload page. The release checklist's current route stops working on that date.
- Pinned listing images can only be corrected by a new release; tag before publishing (or publish from the push run on
  `main`), and keep `docs/images/nuiget.gif` while a 1.18.9 install may still read its local README.
- The repository's `docs:check` constrains README tables (`| Setting | Default | Description |`, `| Command |
  Description |`) and backticked paths; `CONTRIBUTING.md` goes under `.github/` or into `.vscodeignore`.

## 13. Performance and perceived speed

The measured facts come from jsdom scratch tests on the repository's own store and from three CI logs; the refuters
corrected the mechanisms. On every save and operation (the revision changes, so the evaluation reruns) the provisional
chunk replaces the authoritative list: SDK-implicit and property-driven rows vanish and return, counts drop and the
provisional badge flashes for the evaluation's ~450 ms; a Refresh of an unchanged project answers from the evaluator's
per-revision cache. The replacement is a specified and tested invariant (`installed.test.tsx` R5, ARCHITECTURE.md
:440-442, P6.2b), so the fix is a per-id merge that takes additions, versions and removals from the provisional and
keeps the evaluated-only fields, with that test and sentence rewritten (X7). After an operation with `edit.ok` or
`restore.ok` the row turns idle with its old action until the affected key is invalidated and resolved; a
settling-pending state in the slice covers the gap (X31). The provisional tier waits for the `dotnet nuget config paths`
walk spawned on every recomputation whose revision changed (317–1,576 ms on CI); a two-target project's first evaluation
sums to ≈1.3 s; emitting after the cached SDK probe and before the walk keeps ADR-009 and needs no ADR change (X32).
Installed-row icons and authors arrive in one all-or-nothing answer whose enrichment waits for the nuget.org
vulnerability data set; that set measures 750 KB decoded (39 KB gzipped, 606 ids), not the 20 MB the code comment says,
and it expires after one hour, so the stall recurs hourly and after Refresh; the proposal to split the protocol was
refuted, the fix is a core-side warm with a bounded leg so a hung endpoint cannot hold the icons (X33). The sidebar asks
for that metadata and never reads it (X75, DP10). A cold search shows a skeleton until every result is enriched (the
P5.6 record is 2,323 ms from Enter to the first row; CI cold searches 581–1,309 ms against the 1,700 target; opening
P7.2d ahead of its trigger is the owner's call, X34); a new search drops the old results for a skeleton where the
Extensions view keeps the old list under a progress bar (X74). The Installed, transitive and All Projects Updates lists
are not virtualized in practice (X37), `MainShell` subscribes to seven contexts and `Row` is not memoized (X38),
streamed update rows re-sort under the pointer (X76), queued and running operations look alike (X77), and the main
panel's first list shows no busy cue beyond an info badge (X78). In All Projects a project's lite metadata is emitted
only after its evaluation (X43). Measurement gaps: `firstUsefulRender` fires on the authoritative answer and no job
collects the perf lines (H57); UI-job timings are quantized to 250 ms steps plus WebDriver round trips, so the start
must be stamped in page (H58); operation durations in the logs are whole-test times, an upper bound (H59); the bench
covers neither the sections layout nor render counts per chunk (H60); raw HTTP sizes and the metadata answer are
untimed (H71). The bundle headroom figures of §3 are the CI numbers; the local ones in the reports came from a
`node_modules` behind the lockfile (X44, H45).

## 14. Architecture constraints

Most of the IA fixes are webview-only: the keyboard model, Enter and Delete semantics, Restore, Consolidate,
promote-transitive, transitive vulnerability counts, severity on badges, collapse/expand all, hover prefetch, the
search-to-list keys, sidebar vulnerability marks and per-project Update all all have their data on the wire. The native
chrome levers need no protocol change: `webview/context` menus on both surfaces, editor-title actions through
`activeWebviewPanelId`, a view badge; the single-view container hides the view description. A frozen-contract change
(sentinel, advisor ruling, regenerated `docs/protocol.md`, guard records, dependent tests) is needed for: any new
user-facing setting the webviews read (`ExtensionConfig` lives in `domain.ts`; the alternatives are no setting, as the
exemplar has, or per-surface webview state), new row and details fields (downloads are already fetched and dropped;
deprecation of the latest version is in the search response), per-version vulnerability flags, Browse paging, the MB3
found chunk, source editing, `projectPath?` on `ui/viewPackage`, audit sources, solution scope. A new Catalog read
amends ADR-009 and a new resource kind ADR-002 even when the protocol does not change. The procedure dominates the
code, so the plan bundles the contract changes into one or two D6 items of at most one session-day of frozen edits
each, scheduled right after the owner's scope decision. Persisted state is split three ways (globalState through
`layout/*`, workspaceState for the session, webview state for tabs and splits); under All Projects the project-less
reads resolve sources from the first workspace folder (X42).

## 15. Triage of the 2.0.1 candidates

All 48 candidates are bucketed in Appendix C (26 UX items, 2 pre-release blockers, 18 post-release backlog, 2 owner
decisions), none unassigned. The verdicts that change something: DP3 (the icon breaker silencing a source after five
404/410 answers until a sources or workspace invalidation) becomes a pre-release blocker because five main-panel lists
now share the resolver; the release mechanics (a checklist filled for the P10.1 build, no pre-release VSIX packaged by
CI, vsce refusing `--pre-release` on a regular package) are the second blocker; N64 is an owner decision tied to the
floor; MB3 (rows before enrichment) needs a contract change and the perf-baseline rule gates it; SB7 takes the
workbench binding Ctrl/Cmd+DownArrow; MI7/DP8 get the workbench severity colours and MI10's danger button is dropped
(no workbench key); recent searches stay removed (the Extensions search box has no history); V3, SB10 and MI12 are one
header decision; 14 candidates that live only in STATUS notes, the closure-table footer and the release checklist
have no ledger rows; several ledger statuses are stale (S4, N14, N7, N5) and N87 is closed. The refuters narrowed DP3's
blast radius (only never-resolved icons blank; cached icons keep showing) and corrected N64's premise: ExTester runs any
VS Code from 1.90, so a floor of 1.138 is not needed to render the floor under ExTester, but no ExTester route renders
1.85 either. The plan adds rows for N41 and N72 and states a default bucket for the two owner-decides rows.

## 16. The VS Code floor

The owner lifted the hard floor on 2026-10-22: raise 1.85 when a technique the design needs, or a clearly better one,
is blocked. The floor analysis (Appendix E) recommends **staying at 1.85.0**: no Design System rule needs a technique
that Chromium 114, Node 18 or the 1.85 API blocks without a correct fallback. `:has()`, `@container`, `color-mix()`,
`popover`, `text-wrap: balance`, `body.vscode-reduce-motion` (since 1.68) and the `webview/context` menu are all at
the floor; the exported size tokens get literal fallbacks that are the classic values and therefore right on every
host below 1.139; the one floor-tied defect (V15, the scrollbars) is a host limitation at every floor and is fixed
by a `scrollbar-color` declaration that Chromium 114 discards, which the lint must learn to admit (a ~0.5 d
lint-policy item: a progressive-enhancement allowlist with a documented 114 behaviour per entry, the scrollbar pins
updated, one UI-job probe). The chat and language-model API reached Stable in 1.91 and no feature here needs it.
Options if the owner prefers one Chromium generation anyway: 1.90.0 (Electron 29, Chromium 122, Node 20.9; ~1 d
mechanical: `engines`, `@types/vscode` 1.90.0, esbuild `node20`/`chrome122`, `@types/node` 20, the E2E matrix, 16 of
26 lint entries pruned, ADR-000 amended; hosts 1.85–1.89 stay on 1.18.9 or 2.0.0; peers already require 1.87–1.90);
1.110.1, 1.124.0 and 1.129.0 only delete token fallbacks; 1.138.0 is a verification choice (lets the UI job render
the floor and close N64) to decide with N64 at the first 2.0.x release. Publishing order matters: a floor raised
before 2.0.0 is published leaves sub-floor users on 1.18.9 for good, a raise after it leaves them on 2.0.0. No
public VS Code version distribution exists; VS Code auto-updates by default.

## 17. What the design has to decide

Outcome (recorded after this report was written; `docs/design/DECISIONS.md` is the record): round 1 on 2026-10-23
picked composition A, the Extensions view (D-A the 72 px Extensions row, D-B 22 px compact rows, D-C one grouped list
per view, D-D sidebar Enter opens the main panel on the row, D-E in-place success feedback); round 2 picked F1a (the
workbench check box with `checkbox.select*` on a selected row), F2a (disabled text buttons at opacity 0.4), F3a
(Browse rows first under a progress bar) and, against the recommendation of Appendix E, a floor of VS Code 1.90.0
(Chromium 122, Node 20.9). The table below is the question as it was put.

Decisions the owner takes as choices between compositions (round 1) or variants (round 2):

| # | Decision | Alternatives | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-A | Comfortable row anatomy | (a) Extensions row: 72 px, 36 px icon, two lines (name + badges / version · authors), actions inside the band; (b) the 78 px three-line card of D15 as amended | DS-HEIGHT-3, DS-ROW-1/3, the divider rule, every list capture |
| D-B | Compact row height | 22 px (native rows and pane headers) or 24 px (the pointer-target choice) | DS-HEIGHT-1/2, sidebar captures, the small-targets lint |
| D-C | List model per view | one grouped list (one tree per view, the workbench's model: keys cross groups, one tab stop) or one list per section (today) | §8 ids, 7 planned days, the Updates merge |
| D-D | Enter in the sidebar | Enter opens the main panel on the row (today's Ctrl+Enter) or only selects; writes move to Ctrl+Enter, Delete and the menu either way | §5, the sidebar key map, README |
| D-E | Success feedback | no toast for a single success (status bar, in-place state and live region only; warnings and bulk summaries keep their toasts) or the D17 toasts | `feedback.ts`, the capture set |
| D-F | The floor | stay at 1.85.0 (recommended, Appendix E) or 1.90.0 for one Chromium generation | ADR-000, tokens, lint threshold, the E2E matrix |
| D-G | Disabled text buttons | opacity 0.4 (the workbench's text button) or no opacity with `disabledForeground` | DS-COLOR-6, V38 |
| D-H | The row check mark | the 18 px workbench check box as the exemplar, or a 3:1 non-text contrast that the exemplar itself misses | DS-HEIGHT-9, V18, A7 |

Decided by the design-advisor with the closest-to-the-workbench rule and recorded in `docs/design/DECISIONS.md` and
ADRs (both to be created by the plan): follow the host's look (Modern UI by default, classic as fallback) through
aliased tokens and a mirrored setting; the block-gap set and symmetric insets; focus-ring behaviour per look; the
context-menu mechanism (`webview/context`); the icon delivery (generated inline SVG from the pinned codicon release);
the token aliasing (px fallbacks, row heights literal); the explicit look keys in the UI settings (Modern UI on for the
primary goldens, one classic pass); the Select primitive (native wrapper first); the scrollbar progressive enhancement;
and every microcopy rule.

## 18. What the verification left open

The completeness critic (Appendix D) ran against the first, interrupted verification pass; the items it marked
"must" that the rerun did not settle, and what happens to each:

- The Modern UI probe run is done (`probe/out-modern/`, §3); the Design System carries a classic and a Modern UI
  column for the metrics that differ. P0's theme-dump step repeats both runs on the CI lane (ubuntu, DPR 1) and adds
  the missing states (hover, keyboard focus, unfocused list, sidebar 170/200/250, main 220/400/600/800, All Projects
  with a provisional badge, dialogs), so the 27 not-measurable rules and V3 get numbers before any screen item.
- The report-internal ids of the feature-gaps, architecture, performance, accessibility and triage reports are
  crosswalked to the ledger ids in Appendix A's rows (the `sourceIds` column); the colliding rule ids were renamed
  (`MS-1..10` measurement, `LST-1..6` listing).
- Costs are stated in session-days with the rate range (0.52 h per planned day measured over P0–P8, 0.76 h per P9
  item measured, 1.1 h per planned UI day assumed by the brief); option B of the component base is 8.5–12 d.
- Bundle headroom uses one base, the CI build at `e4d6e97`.
- Not done, carried into the plan: the context-menu runtime path proven in the E2E (1.85.0) job (first vertical
  slice); the codicon font route costed beside inline SVG (component-base item); competitor user signal from the
  NuGet Gallery and NuGet GUI issue trackers (a research follow-up, letter W); the store slices and `main.css:317-590`
  audited with the same verdict table (component item); performance timed in Chromium instead of jsdom (P0 harness);
  pixelmatch executed on two capture sets and one controlled vision-judge trial (P0); exemplar webviews screenshotted
  in the ExTester profile (design phase); forced-colours and zoom modalities (accessibility item).

## Appendix A. Findings ledger (299 rows)

Ids extend `docs/plans/findings-ledger.md` (letters X, V from V6, A, G, W, H; never reused or renumbered). "Verification" is the three-lens refuter result on the decision-relevant rows: `holds`, `corrected` (the correction follows), `refuted`, `unverifiable`; rows that were not decision-relevant were not refuted. Evidence cites the research reports' path:line or URL; the reports themselves are in the research bundle (`docs/research/2026-10-23-ux-research/` once committed).

Rows per letter: H 95, V 66, X 82, A 21, G 21, W 14. Verification tally over the decision-relevant rows: {"holds":17,"corrected":155,"refuted":1,"unverifiable":2,"missing":0}.

The protocol's sidebarSectionSplit has no reader or writer in either webview. No WebviewPanelSerializer is registered, so the main panel is not restored after a window reload. The sidebar's webview state is saved with the view. Section collapse, filters and per-surface density can persist with no contract change. **Proposal:** Keep new surface-local preferences in webview state. Consider a panel serializer (host-only) if restoring the panel after a reload matters. | src/host/handlers.ts:246; src/protocol/domain.ts:337 | **not refuted (not decision-relevant)** |

## Appendix B. Design rules bound to the probe (72 rules)

Workbench reference values come from the ExTester probe on VS Code 1.142.0 (Dark Modern, classic look, DPR 2, CSS px); the nUIget values from the same probe's capture of `main-installed-single`, `main-updates` and `sidebar-sections`. `not-measurable` means the probe lacked the state (hover, keyboard focus, narrow widths, All Projects).

## Appendix D. Completeness critic

The sweep covers every dimension of the brief, but its verification layer is incomplete and misreported: verify-result.json tallies 33 "missing" verdicts (every decision-relevant claim of performance, visual-audit and candidates-triage) and critic/floor/modern are null, while report-narrative.md says "54 refuters, refuted none" and cites Appendices D-F that do not exist. On disk, batch 16 (performance) was refuted in all three lenses but never merged (its consequence lens refutes X33 and leaves X34 to the owner; two of its files predate the assembly because the workflow folds only in-memory results), batch 18 exists as a counter JSON plus Markdown drafts, and batch 17 never ran. Second, nobody has measured the look a real VS Code 1.142 user sees: the probe and the CI captures are classic because ExTester injects workbench.experimental.modernUI: false and --disable-experiments (codeUtil.js:196-197, browser.js:182; --code_settings merge on top, :160), so the narrative's "target the classic workbench, measured on 1.142.0" rests on ExTester's VS Code; all Modern UI values are CSS-read. Third, 27 of 72 bound rules are not measurable from the probe (no hover, focus, unfocused-list, narrow, empty/error, dialog or All Projects state; the V3 state does not exist in it), three rules bind to premises the exemplar itself fails (DS-GRID-2 {4,8} vs measured 5/10; DS-INSET-2 symmetry vs 5/12/6/20; DS-FOCUS-3 vs the classic list's click outline), five pass by absence of the state that would fail them (DS-OVERFLOW-2 for V3, DS-COLOR-2, DS-ROW-2/4, DS-COPY-3), and every bound workbench number is macOS DPR 2 while the gate runs on ubuntu DPR 1. Fourth, ids drift: feature-gaps.md numbers G1-G21 differently from the ledger (and architecture.md cites the report's numbering), performance/accessibility/triage use X-n, H1-H8, F1-F17 that the ledger renumbers, listing R1-R6 collide with measurement R1-R10 (gen-ledger's Object.fromEntries keeps one set), and the letter scheme (X, H) duplicates or overrides the repository ledger's U, P, D, T and V definitions; H is a catch-all holding architecture, component-base, listing and bookkeeping rows. Fifth, competitor user signal (NuGet Gallery's 92 issues and 37 reviews) fell between the user-signal and feature-gaps dimensions. Costs come in three units and three rates with option B's prose (8-11) contradicting its own table (8.5-12.0), and bundle headroom is quoted from two builds (CI 551,394/333,913 vs stale local 548,943/333,252). V3 is the weakest V mapping (no probe state, by-proxy rules, the catching rule passes), then V4 (three measurement frames, no proximity rule bound, the workbench header has the opposite asymmetry), V5 (rule premises the exemplar fails), V1 (its height exemplar was never measured; compared against the 35 px editor tab strip instead of the Settings scope tabs), while V2 is sound but the shipped gate tolerates 8 px. Load-bearing facts were re-verified first-hand: the probe's 79/72/22/26/35 px numbers, G1's code path (vulnerability.ts:107-122, enricher.ts:71-73), the 1.85 contextmenu handler's defaultPrevented-only check, the absence of onContextMenu in main-panel lists, ExTester's ChromeDriver >= 115 throw (VS Code >= 1.90 only, which also voids N64 option A under ExTester), and the icon match counts (5 pre-redraw, 5 current, 13 none of 23).

## Appendix E. VS Code floor options

Keep engines.vscode at ^1.85.0 for the UX overhaul: no Design System rule needs a technique that Chromium 114, Node 18 or the 1.85 API blocks without a correct fallback. The one floor-tied defect (V15: VS Code's inherited `html { scrollbar-color }` disables the 10 px WebKit scrollbar rules on every host from 1.90) is fixed by a `scrollbar-color` declaration that Chromium 114 discards while the rest of the block applies; what blocks it is the lint (webview-css-baseline.test.ts:37-38) and the pinned declarations (webview-stylesheets.test.ts:133-137), not the floor, and on Chromium >= 121 the 10 px look still needs the WebKit rules plus a `scrollbar-color: auto` reset, so no floor removes a line of the fix. Corrections to the reports, verified in microsoft/vscode history: the webview size-token export (63cc68e, 2026-02-04) first ships in 1.110.1 (tag 1.110.0 does not exist) with only cornerRadius.*, bodyFontSize*, codiconFontSize and strokeThickness (release/1.110 baseSizes.ts has 11 ids), not the whole ramp as component-base.md says; codiconFontSize.compact first in 1.122.0; the 14 spacing tokens (d97df0a, 2026-06-05) first in 1.124.0 (release/1.123 lacks them), not 1.130 as report-narrative.md says; the fontSize.heading/body/label ramp and fontWeight.* (dd766a81f, 2026-07-07) first in 1.129.0. body.vscode-reduce-motion was first set in 1.68 (absent at 1.67) and is present at 1.85. The chat/language-model API reached Stable in 1.91 (1.90 notes: Insiders only). :has(), @container, color-mix(), popover, text-wrap: balance and webview/context are all at the floor. Hosts: 1.85 = Electron 25.9.7 (Chromium 114.0.5735.289, Node 18.15.0); 1.89 = 28.2.8 (120, Node 18.18.2); 1.90 = 29.4.0 (122.0.6261.156, Node 20.9.0: the lowest release with both Chromium >= 121 and Node 20); 1.110.1 = 39.6.0 (142, Node 22.22.0); 1.124.0 = 42.2.0 (148, Node 24.15.0); 1.129.0 = 42.6.0 (148, Node 24.18.0); 1.138.0 (ExTester 8.28.1 minimum) = 42.10.0 (148, Node 24.18.1); 1.141.0 (stable 1.143.1 minus 2) = 43.7.7 (150, Node 24.21.0). User base: nUIget has 83 installs / 921 downloads and 25 published versions, newest 1.18.9 (2026-10-10), no 2.x published; VS Code installs the newest version whose engines.vscode matches, so a floor raised before 2.0.0 publishes leaves sub-floor users on 1.18.9 for good, while 2.0.0 published at ^1.85 first leaves them on 2.0.0 (owner decision on the order). No public VS Code version-distribution data exists; VS Code auto-updates by default; peers already require 1.87-1.90 (versionlens >= 1.90 with 2.55 M installs, dependi ^1.87, nuget-gallery ^1.87). check:vsix does enforce @types/vscode <= engines.vscode (vsce readManifest -> validateManifestForPackaging). @types/vscode tags after 1.110 are sparse (1.115, 1.116, 1.118, 1.120, 1.125, 1.134, 1.136, 1.137, 1.138, 1.140), so a 1.124 floor pins 1.120.0 and a 1.129 floor pins 1.125.0.

**Recommendation:** Floor 1.85.0 (stay). The actual change is a lint-policy item (~0.5 d): let webview-css-baseline.test.ts admit named progressive-enhancement declarations whose Chromium-114 behaviour is documented (first entry: scrollbar-color, with the existing ::-webkit-scrollbar block as the 114 path), update the scrollbar pins in webview-stylesheets.test.ts:133-137, and probe the result once in the UI job on Chromium 150 (no job renders 114, N64). Token aliases keep literal fallbacks, which are the classic values and therefore correct on every host below 1.139 (Modern UI default); raising to 1.124/1.129 only deletes fallbacks. Condition: if the UI-job probe shows that neither `scrollbar-color: auto` on the root nor `scrollbar-color: <thumb> transparent` restores 10 px token scrollbars under VS Code's @layer vscode-default stylesheet, V15 is a host limitation at every floor and the recommendation stands. Owner-preference fallback: 1.90.0 (Chromium 122, Node 20.9.0) if one Chromium generation and keep-alive on every host are wanted; it prunes 16 of 26 lint entries, retargets esbuild to node20/chrome122, repins @types/vscode 1.90.0 and @types-host/node to npm:@types/node@20.9.5, moves the E2E matrix to 1.90.0, and amends ADR-000 behind the sentinel, at the price of hosts 1.85-1.89 (Dec 2023 to May 2024) staying on 1.18.9 or 2.0.0; peers already dropped them. Nothing in the reports justifies 1.110, 1.124, 1.129 or the stable-minus-2 1.141; a floor of 1.138 is a verification choice (lets the UI job render the floor and close N64), to be decided with N64 at the first 2.0.x release, not with this design.

## Appendix F. Modern UI verification

**What it is:** Setting `workbench.experimental.modernUI` (boolean; `LayoutSettings.MODERN_UI`, layoutService.ts:54 at release/1.142). `ModernUIContribution.isEnabled()` is `getValue(...) === true` with no quality/platform/telemetry gate (modernUI.contribution.ts:154-155 at release/1.142); `applyTo` toggles `modern-ui`, `modern-ui-compact`, `modern-ui-tabs`, `modern-ui-connected-editor-tabs` (editor.ts:32), `modern-ui-notifications-dialogs`, `modern-ui-uppercase-view-headers` on each workbench container, never on body or in a webview; JS metrics `MODERN_UI_PANE_HEADER_SIZE = 28` (default 22), `MODERN_UI_SCROLLBAR_SIZE = 8` (DEFAULT_SCROLLBAR_SIZE = 10), compact notification rows; `paneHeaders` is layout-affecting so toggling relayouts live. Module catalogue (lines 80-94): activityBar, commandCenter, editorBorder, fontRamp, keyboardFocusOnly, padding, paneHeaders, roundedCorners, sashHandles, scrollShadows, shadows, statusBar, tabs, titlebar, notificationsDialogs; no codicon module. Values read in the 1.142.0 stable CSS: `.monaco-pane-view{--pane-header-size:22px}` vs `.modern-ui .monaco-pane-view{--pane-header-size:var(--vscode-spacing-size280,28px)}`; title `uppercase` 11/700 vs `fontSize-label1` 12 / `fontWeight-semiBold` 600 / `capitalize` (uppercase only with `workbench.experimental.modernUIUppercaseViewHeaders`); pane header border none + inset separator, `cornerRadius-small`, hover `list.hoverBackground`, margins/padding `size40`; `.modern-ui .monaco-list .monaco-list-row:not(.separator){border-radius:cornerRadius-small}` and rows inset `size40` (compact `size20`); `.modern-ui.monaco-workbench .part>.title{height:32px}` (classic 35); count badge `label3` 10 px; corner tiers controls 4 / inner 6 / outer 8; floating cards with gaps, compact density (`window.density.layout: compact`) zeroes margins and radii; `.webview-overlay-content` gets `cornerRadius-large` 8 px (0 under compact). Companion settings: `window.density.layout` (default\|compact), `workbench.experimental.modernUIUppercaseViewHeaders` (false), `workbench.experimental.modernUIEditorTabStyle` (connected\|pill, since 1.139), `workbench.modernUIFrostedGlass`(+Opacity). Not changed: codicon glyphs/sizes (`codiconFontSize` 16 / compact 12 are constants; the 2025-10 and 2026-02 redraws are independent), the exported size-token values, theme colours (Modern UI adds registry colour ids `surface.*`, `modernTab.*`, `modernEditorTab.*`, `modernPanel.border`, which reach webviews with the setting off too: 21 keys in the probe's dark-modern.json). `.monaco-text-button` (12 px/16 lh/4 px radius) and `.quick-input-widget` (`cornerRadius-xLarge` 12 px) are ungated. Exported size tokens (dark-modern.json, constant across all six themes): spacing sizeNone 0, size20 2, size40 4, size60 6, size80 8, size100 10, size120 12, size160 16, size200 20, size240 24, size280 28, size320 32, size360 36, size400 40 px; cornerRadius xSmall 2, small 4, medium 6, large 8, xLarge 12, circle 9999; fontSize heading1 26, heading2 18, heading3 13, body1 13, body2 11, label1 12, label2 11, label3 10; fontWeight regular 400, semiBold 600; codiconFontSize 16, codiconFontSize-compact 12; strokeThickness 1; no lineHeight tokens; deprecated bodyFontSize 13/12/11; `--vscode-font-size` hard-coded 13px. Registered with `sizeForAllThemes` (baseSizes.ts), resolved by `defaults[theme.type]` only (sizeUtils.ts:231-238), exported by themeing.ts:72-79 since release/1.110 (commit 63cc68e088, 2026-02-04; absent at release/1.109). No signal of Modern UI reaches a webview (body class is only vscode-light/dark/high-contrast(-light)).

**Rollout:** 1.129: `default: false`, tag experimental, release notes "Modern UI preview (Experimental)… enabled by default in Insiders builds". 1.136–1.138: default false with `experiment: { mode: 'auto' }`. Commits eaeb8d9be2 "settings: enable Modern UI Update by default" and 88f4cfe9f8 "add experiment mode" on 2026-09-21; tag 1.139.0 (2026-09-22), 1.140.0 (2026-09-30), 1.143.0 (2026-10-20) and 1.143.1 all compile `default: true, experiment: { mode: 'auto' }`; the stable 1.142.0 binary on this Mac (built 2026-10-15, quality stable, no product.json configurationDefaults) compiles `default:!0, experiment:{mode:"auto"}`. Experiment mode 'auto' (configurationRegistry.ts:299-305) lets the TAS treatment `config.workbench.experimental.modernUI` override the default whenever it changes; with no assignment (`shouldOverride(undefined)` is false, configurationService.ts:1495-1503; experiments disabled → undefined, assignmentService.ts:331-333) the code default stands. Release notes 1.139–1.143 never announce the flip (1.141 only says frosted glass needs modernUI and the tab style is experimental); the VS Code blog "Modernizing the VS Code UI" (Lee Murray, 2026-10-15) states: experimental in Stable → default in Insiders → controlled Stable experiment for a subset → enabled by default, classic UI available via the setting during the transition, removal date unset. So in 1.142/1.143 stable Modern UI is the compiled default and is user-visible by default unless the experiment service assigns `false` to that user or the user turned it off; what fraction currently gets `false` is not verifiable from any source opened. Newest stable per the update API on 2026-10-23: 1.143.1.

**The probe rendered:** Classic. ExTester 8.28.1 writes `"workbench.experimental.modernUI": false` and `"workbench.enableExperiments": false` (node_modules/vscode-extension-tester/out/util/codeUtil.js:196-197) merged under the repository's src/test/ui/settings.json (browser.js:129-151, custom spread last at :146) and launches with `--disable-telemetry --disable-experiments` (browser.js:181-182); the generated /tmp/nuiget-ui/settings/User/settings.json on this Mac carries `"workbench.experimental.modernUI": false`, which beats the 1.142.0 binary's compiled default `true`. Measurements agree: workbench/explorer.json pane-header rect h 22, `.title` 11px / 700 / uppercase, line-height 22, radius 0; monaco-list-row h 22; `composite title` h 35; probe-workbench-explorer.png shows EXPLORER / E2E-WORKSPACE / OUTLINE / TIMELINE in capitals with flush square panels. The quick-input-widget radius 12 px is `cornerRadius-xLarge` ungated in the 1.142.0 CSS and not a Modern UI tell. The pin entered ExTester in commit 4fc75b9bc8 (2026-09-11): absent at tag v8.26.0, present at v8.27.0 (2026-09-14) and v8.28.1.

**CI rendered:** Classic, same cause. Run 39025149785 (head c50d335f71f81b0ecb3e12a867867d0928117226, labs/2.0, 2026-10-17) locks vscode-extension-tester 8.28.1 in package-lock.json, so the runner's generated settings also carried `workbench.experimental.modernUI: false`; the job log says "Downloading VS Code: 1.140.0 / stable" and warns that 1.142.0 is the latest stable. main-installed-single.png shows EXPLORER / E2E-WORKSPACE / OUTLINE / TIMELINE in capitals, side bar, editor and status bar flush with square corners and the classic 35 px tab band; sidebar-sections.png shows the same chrome around nUIget's own uppercase section headers. 1.140.0's source default is `true` (tag 1.140.0:836), so the classic look came from ExTester's injected user setting, not from an experiment assignment (experiments were disabled).

**Recommendation:** Target "follow the host, classic as fallback" (the GitLens pattern), not classic-only or Modern-UI-only: the workbench is the exemplar and now has two user-chosen looks (code default Modern UI since 1.139, classic via the setting and forced by ExTester), so closest-to-the-workbench is only satisfiable by matching whichever look the host shows. Three parts: (1) alias tokens.css spacing/radius/type to the exported sizes with literal fallbacks (`var(--vscode-spacing-size160, 16px)` etc.) — identical pixels today since the values are constants, future-proof, a documented mapping; the tokens lint (webview-tokens.test.ts:49-65) does not forbid it and the pxSpacing lint exempts tokens.css, so V9's "extend the lint" is unneeded, a rule requiring the alias form is what to add; (2) mirror `workbench.experimental.modernUI`, `window.density.layout`, `workbench.experimental.modernUIUppercaseViewHeaders` from the host (as readExtensionConfig already reads workbench.tree.indent, services.ts:97) onto the webview root as data attributes — via renderWebviewHtml's dataset (no contract change, stale until re-render) or as ExtensionConfig fields in src/protocol/domain.ts (live via config/changed, frozen contract, sentinel; fold into the one D6 item); (3) switch only the metrics that differ under `[data-modern-ui]`: section header 22→28 (size280), header type 11/700/uppercase→label1/semiBold/capitalize (uppercase under the uppercase attribute), hover tint, row radius 0→small, row inset 0→size40 (compact size20), toolbars 35→32, scrollbar 10→8, badge 11→label3; buttons, inputs, popover radii are ungated and identical in both looks. The 1.85 floor is not touched by any of this. Harness consequence: the look is decided by one key in src/test/ui/settings.json because ExTester spreads it last over its defaults (browser.js:146) and modernUI is not flag-shadowed; today the goldens pin classic only implicitly through ExTester's default. Write `"workbench.experimental.modernUI": true` (plus `"window.density.layout": "default"`, `"workbench.experimental.modernUIUppercaseViewHeaders": false`) explicitly as the primary goldens (the stable default look), and keep one classic pass — cheapest as a runtime toggle inside the UI suite (the contribution relayouts on configuration change; not verified in a run), otherwise a second --code_settings file/job. rules-bound.md/json currently bind classic values only (22 / 11 px 700 uppercase / 35 / radius 0): re-run probe/zz-probe.ui.test.ts with the key on, and record `.monaco-workbench`'s className in `__document`, before binding any M value. Record the VS Code and ExTester versions per capture set and consider pinning --code_version instead of max (Dependabot auto-merged ExTester bumps move vscode-max). Keep `--disable-experiments` / `workbench.enableExperiments: false`: they make the default deterministic.
